</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Pope Benedict XV</b> (<a href="/wiki/Ecclesiastical_Latin" title="Ecclesiastical Latin">Latin</a>: <i>Benedictus XV</i>; <a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Benedetto XV</i>), born <b>Giacomo Paolo Giovanni Battista della Chiesa</b><sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> (<small>Italian:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="it-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Italian" title="Help:IPA/Italian">[ˈdʒa:komo ˈpa:olo dʒoˈvanni batˈtista della ˈkjɛ:za]</a></span>; 21 November 1854&#160;&#8211;&#32;22 January 1922), was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> from 1914 until his death in January 1922. His pontificate was largely overshadowed by <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a> and its political, social, and humanitarian consequences in Europe.
</p><p>Between 1846 and 1903, the Catholic Church had experienced two of its longest pontificates in history up to that point. Together <a href="/wiki/Pius_IX" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius IX">Pius IX</a> and <a href="/wiki/Leo_XIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Leo XIII">Leo XIII</a> ruled for a total of 57 years. In 1914, the College of Cardinals chose della Chiesa at the relatively young age of 59 at the outbreak of World War I, which he labeled "<a href="/wiki/The_suicide_of_civilized_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="The suicide of civilized Europe">the suicide of civilized Europe</a>". The war and its consequences were the main focus of Benedict XV. He immediately declared the neutrality of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> and attempted from that perspective to mediate peace in 1916 and 1917. Both sides rejected his initiatives. German Protestants rejected any "Papal Peace" as insulting. The French politician <a href="/wiki/Georges_Clemenceau" title="Georges Clemenceau">Georges Clemenceau</a> regarded the Vatican initiative as being anti-French.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_380_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_380-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Having failed with diplomatic initiatives, Benedict XV focused on humanitarian efforts to lessen the impacts of the war, such as attending prisoners of war, the exchange of wounded soldiers and food deliveries to needy populations in Europe. After the war, he repaired the difficult relations with France, which re-established relations with the Vatican in 1921. During his pontificate, relations with Italy improved as well, as Benedict XV now permitted Catholic politicians led by Don <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Sturzo" title="Luigi Sturzo">Luigi Sturzo</a> to participate in national Italian politics.
</p><p>In 1917, Benedict XV promulgated the <a href="/wiki/1917_Code_of_Canon_Law" title="1917 Code of Canon Law">Code of Canon Law</a>, which was released on 27 May, the creation of which he had prepared with <a href="/wiki/Eugenio_Pacelli" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugenio Pacelli">Eugenio Pacelli</a> (the future Pope Pius XII) and <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Gasparri" title="Pietro Gasparri">Pietro Gasparri</a> during the pontificate of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a>. The new Code of Canon Law is considered to have stimulated religious life and activities throughout the Church.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_382_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_382-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> He named Gasparri to be his <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a> and personally consecrated <a href="/wiki/Nuncio" title="Nuncio">Nuncio</a> Pacelli on 13 May 1917 as Archbishop. World War I caused great damage to Catholic missions throughout the world. Benedict XV revitalized these activities, asking in <i>Maximum illud</i> for Catholics throughout the world to participate. For that, he has been referred to as the "Pope of Missions". His last concern was the emerging persecution of the Catholic Church in <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Russia" class="mw-redirect" title="Soviet Russia">Soviet Russia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Famine" title="Famine">famine</a> there after the revolution. Benedict XV was devoted to the <a href="/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Blessed Virgin Mary">Blessed Virgin Mary</a> and authorized the Feast of <a href="/wiki/Mary,_Mediatrix_of_all_Graces" class="mw-redirect" title="Mary, Mediatrix of all Graces">Mary, Mediatrix of all Graces</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-AAS_1921,_345_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AAS_1921,_345-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After seven years in office, Pope Benedict XV died on 22 January 1922 after battling pneumonia since the start of that month. He was buried in the grottos of Saint Peter's Basilica. With his diplomatic skills and his openness towards modern society, "he gained respect for himself and the papacy."<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_382_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_382-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:187px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg/185px-Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg" decoding="async" width="185" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg/277px-Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg/369px-Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg 2x" data-file-width="655" data-file-height="833" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedictxvfamilyresidence.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The birthplace of Giacomo della Chiesa in Pegli</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:187px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedictxc1868.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Benedictxc1868.jpg/185px-Benedictxc1868.jpg" decoding="async" width="185" height="229" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Benedictxc1868.jpg/278px-Benedictxc1868.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Benedictxc1868.jpg/370px-Benedictxc1868.jpg 2x" data-file-width="541" data-file-height="671" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedictxc1868.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Giacomo in 1866 at the age of twelve</div></div></div>
<p>Giacomo della Chiesa was born prematurely at <a href="/wiki/Pegli" title="Pegli">Pegli</a>, a suburb of <a href="/wiki/Genoa" title="Genoa">Genoa</a>, Italy, the third son and sixth child of <a href="/wiki/Marchese" class="mw-redirect" title="Marchese">Marchese</a> Giuseppe della Chiesa (1821–1892) and his wife <a href="/wiki/Marchesa" class="mw-redirect" title="Marchesa">Marchesa</a> Giovanna Migliorati (1827–1904).<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Genealogy findings report that his father's side produced <a href="/wiki/Pope_Callixtus_II" title="Pope Callixtus II">Pope Callixtus II</a> and also claimed descent from <a href="/wiki/Berengar_II_of_Italy" title="Berengar II of Italy">Berengar II of Italy</a> and that his maternal family produced <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_VII" title="Pope Innocent VII">Pope Innocent VII</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He is also a descendant of <a href="/wiki/Antonio_della_Chiesa" title="Antonio della Chiesa">Blessed Antonio della Chiesa</a>. His brother, Giovanni Antonio Della Chiesa, married the niece of Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Jacobini" title="Angelo Jacobini">Angelo Jacobini</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Due to his premature birth, Giacomo was left with a limp and completed much of his early education at home.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His wish to become a priest was rejected early on by his father, who insisted on a legal career for his son. At age 21 he acquired a doctorate in Law on 2 August 1875. He had attended the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Genoa" title="University of Genoa">University of Genoa</a>, which after the unification of Italy was largely dominated by <a href="/wiki/Anti-Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-Catholic">anti-Catholic</a> and anti-clerical politics. With his doctorate in Law and at legal age, he again asked his father for permission to study for the priesthood, which was now reluctantly granted. He insisted, however, that his son conduct his theological studies in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, not in <a href="/wiki/Genoa" title="Genoa">Genoa</a>, so that he would not end up as a village priest or provincial <a href="/wiki/Monsignore" class="mw-redirect" title="Monsignore">monsignore</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Della Chiesa entered the <a href="/wiki/Collegio_Capranica" class="mw-redirect" title="Collegio Capranica">Collegio Capranica</a> and was there in Rome when, in 1878, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a> died and was followed by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a>. The new pope received the students of the Capranica in private audience only a few days after his coronation. Shortly thereafter, della Chiesa was ordained a priest by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Raffaele_Monaco_La_Valletta" title="Raffaele Monaco La Valletta">Raffaele Monaco La Valletta</a> on 21 December 1878 at the <a href="/wiki/Archbasilica_of_Saint_John_Lateran" title="Archbasilica of Saint John Lateran">Lateran basilica</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Miranda_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Miranda-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 1878 until 1883 he studied at the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Ecclesiastical_Academy" title="Pontifical Ecclesiastical Academy">Pontificia Accademia dei Nobili Ecclesiastici</a> in Rome. It was there, on every Thursday, that students were required to defend a research paper, to which cardinals and high members of the Roman Curia were invited. <a href="/wiki/Mariano_Rampolla" title="Mariano Rampolla">Mariano Rampolla</a>, then Secretary for Oriental Affairs of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Evangelization_of_Peoples" title="Congregation for the Evangelization of Peoples">Congregation for the Propagation of the Faith</a>, took note of him and furthered his entry in the diplomatic service of the Vatican in 1882, where he was employed by Rampolla as a secretary and in January 1883 accompanied nuncio Rampolla to <a href="/wiki/Madrid" title="Madrid">Madrid</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Miranda_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Miranda-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> When Rampolla subsequently was appointed <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a>, della Chiesa followed him. During these years, della Chiesa helped negotiate the resolution of a dispute between Germany and Spain over the <a href="/wiki/Caroline_Islands" title="Caroline Islands">Caroline Islands</a> as well as organising relief during a <a href="/wiki/Cholera" title="Cholera">cholera</a> epidemic.
</p><p>His ambitious mother, Marchesa della Chiesa, is said to have been discontented with the career of her son, cornering Rampolla with the words, that, in her opinion, Giacomo was not properly recognised in the Vatican. Rampolla allegedly replied, "Signora, your son will take only a few steps, but they will be gigantic ones."<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Just after <a href="/wiki/Leo_XIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Leo XIII">Leo XIII</a>'s death in 1903, Rampolla tried to make della Chiesa the secretary of the conclave, but the Sacred College elected <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Merry_del_Val" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Merry del Val">Rafael Merry del Val</a>, a conservative young prelate, the first sign that Rampolla would not be the next Pope. When Cardinal Rampolla had to leave his post with the election of his opponent <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a>, and was succeeded by Merry del Val, della Chiesa was retained in his post.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Bologna">Bologna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Bologna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:232px;"><a href="/wiki/File:PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg/230px-PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg" decoding="async" width="230" height="245" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg/345px-PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg/460px-PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1220" data-file-height="1301" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:PiusXbenedict_XV.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a> consecrates his future successor Pope Benedict XV as Archbishop Giacomo della Chiesa in the Vatican on 22 December 1907</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Archbishop">Archbishop</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Archbishop">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Della Chiesa's association with Rampolla, the architect of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a>'s (1878–1903) foreign policy, made his position in the <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretariat of State">Secretariat of State</a> under the new pontificate somewhat uncomfortable. Italian papers announced that on 15 April 1907 the papal nuncio <a href="/wiki/Aristide_Rinaldini" title="Aristide Rinaldini">Aristide Rinaldini</a> in Madrid would be replaced by della Chiesa, who had worked there before. <a href="/wiki/Pius_X" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius X">Pius X</a>, chuckling over the journalist's knowledge, commented, "Unfortunately, the paper forgot to mention whom I nominated as the next <a href="/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Bologna" class="mw-redirect" title="Archdiocese of Bologna">Archbishop of Bologna</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The Vatican had supposedly "gone so far as to make out the papers naming him the papal nuncio, but [della Chiesa] refused to accept them".<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> On 18 December 1907, in the presence of his family, the diplomatic corps, numerous bishops and cardinals, and his friend Rampolla, he received the episcopal consecration from <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a> himself. The Pope donated his own <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_ring" class="mw-redirect" title="Episcopal ring">episcopal ring</a> and <a href="/wiki/Crosier" title="Crosier">crosier</a> to the new bishop and spent much time with the della Chiesa family on the following day.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> On 23 February 1908, della Chiesa took possession of his new diocese, which included 700,000 persons, 750 priests, as well as 19 male and 78 female <a href="/wiki/Religious_institute" title="Religious institute">religious institutes</a>. In the episcopal seminary, some 25 teachers educated 120 students preparing for the priesthood.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As bishop, he visited all parishes, making a special effort to see the smaller ones in the mountains which could only be accessed on horse-back. He always saw preaching as the main obligation of a bishop. He usually gave two or more sermons a day during his visitations. His emphasis was on cleanliness inside all churches and chapels and on saving money wherever possible, for he said, "Let us save to give to the poor."<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> A meeting of all priests in a <a href="/wiki/Synod" title="Synod">synod</a> had to be postponed at the wish of the Vatican considering ongoing changes in <a href="/wiki/Canon_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon Law">Canon Law</a>. Numerous churches were built or restored. He personally originated a major reform of the educational orientation of the seminary, adding more science courses and classic education to the <a href="/wiki/Curriculum" title="Curriculum">curriculum</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> He organized pilgrimages to Marian shrines in <a href="/wiki/Loreto_(AN)" class="mw-redirect" title="Loreto (AN)">Loreto</a> and <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Lourdes" title="Our Lady of Lourdes">Lourdes</a> at the 50th anniversary of the apparition.<sup id="cite_ref-De_Waal_121_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Waal_121-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The unexpected death of his friend, supporter and mentor Rampolla on 16 December 1913<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> was a major blow to della Chiesa, who was one of the beneficiaries of his will.<sup id="cite_ref-De_Waal_121_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Waal_121-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinal">Cardinal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Cardinal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedicthorse.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Benedicthorse.jpg/200px-Benedicthorse.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="239" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Benedicthorse.jpg/300px-Benedicthorse.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Benedicthorse.jpg/400px-Benedicthorse.jpg 2x" data-file-width="667" data-file-height="797" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedicthorse.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Archbishop della Chiesa on pastoral visit in 1910</div></div></div>
<p>It was a custom that the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop" title="Archbishop">Archbishop</a> of <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a> would be made a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinal</a> in one of the coming <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">consistories</a>. In Bologna this was surely expected of della Chiesa as well, since, in previous years, either cardinals were named as archbishops, or archbishops as cardinals soon thereafter.<sup id="cite_ref-De_Waal_110_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Waal_110-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pius_X" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius X">Pius X</a> did not follow this tradition and left della Chiesa waiting for almost seven years. When a delegation from Bologna visited him to ask for della Chiesa's promotion to the College of Cardinals, he jokingly replied by making fun of his own family name, Sarto (meaning "tailor"), for he said, "Sorry, but a Sarto has not been found yet to make the cardinal's robe."<sup id="cite_ref-De_Waal_110_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-De_Waal_110-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Some suspected that Pius X or persons close to him did not want to have two Rampollas in the College of Cardinals.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg/200px-Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="283" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg/300px-Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg/400px-Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1152" data-file-height="1632" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedikt_XV._als_KardinalJS.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cardinal Della Chiesa in 1914</div></div></div>
<p>Cardinal Rampolla died on 16 December 1913. On 25 May 1914, della Chiesa was created a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinal</a>, becoming <a href="/wiki/Cardinal-Priest" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal-Priest">Cardinal-Priest</a> of the <i><a href="/wiki/Titular_church" title="Titular church">titulus</a></i> <i><a href="/wiki/Santi_Quattro_Coronati" title="Santi Quattro Coronati">Santi Quattro Coronati</a>,</i> which before him was occupied by <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Respighi" title="Pietro Respighi">Pietro Respighi</a>. When the new cardinal tried to return to Bologna after the consistory in Rome, an unrelated socialist, anti-monarchic and anti-Catholic uprising began to take place in Central Italy. This was accompanied by a general strike, the looting and destruction of churches, telephone connections and railway buildings, and a proclamation of a secular republic. In Bologna itself, citizens and the Catholic Church opposed such developments successfully. The Socialists overwhelmingly won the following regional elections with great majorities.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As World War I approached, the question was hotly discussed in Italy as to which side to be on. Officially, Italy was still in an <a href="/wiki/Political_alliance" class="mw-redirect" title="Political alliance">alliance</a> with Germany and <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>. However, in the <a href="/wiki/Tyrol_(state)" title="Tyrol (state)">Tirol</a>, an integral part of Austria which was mostly German-speaking, the southern part, the province of <a href="/wiki/Trento" title="Trento">Trento</a>, was exclusively Italian-speaking. The clergy of Bologna was not totally free from nationalistic fervor either. Therefore, in his capacity as Archbishop, on the outbreak of <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, della Chiesa made a speech on the Church's position and duties, emphasizing the need for <a href="/wiki/Neutral_country" title="Neutral country">neutrality</a>, promoting peace and the easing of suffering.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Pontificate">Pontificate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Pontificate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bene15coronation1914.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Bene15coronation1914.jpg/300px-Bene15coronation1914.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="192" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Bene15coronation1914.jpg/450px-Bene15coronation1914.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Bene15coronation1914.jpg/600px-Bene15coronation1914.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1140" data-file-height="729" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bene15coronation1914.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Coronation of Pope Benedict XV in 1914</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Benedict XV</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:C_o_a_Benedictus_XV.svg" class="image"><img alt="C o a Benedictus XV.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/C_o_a_Benedictus_XV.svg/60px-C_o_a_Benedictus_XV.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="70" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/C_o_a_Benedictus_XV.svg/90px-C_o_a_Benedictus_XV.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/C_o_a_Benedictus_XV.svg/120px-C_o_a_Benedictus_XV.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1006" data-file-height="1179" /></a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data">None</td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Election_to_the_Papacy">Election to the Papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Election to the Papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_1914" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, 1914">Papal conclave, 1914</a></div>
<p>Following the death of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pius X</a>, the resulting conclave opened at the end of August 1914. The war would clearly be the dominant issue of the new pontificate, so the cardinals' priority was to choose a man with great diplomatic experience. Thus on 3 September 1914, della Chiesa, despite having been a cardinal only three months, was elected pope, taking the name of Benedict XV. He chose the name in honour of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XIV" title="Pope Benedict XIV">Pope Benedict XIV</a>, who was also archbishop from Bologna.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Upon being elected pope, he was also formally the Grand Master of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Holy_Sepulchre" title="Order of the Holy Sepulchre">Equestrian Order of the Holy Sepulchre of Jerusalem</a>, <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">prefect of the Supreme Sacred Congregation of the Holy Office</a> and <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_Bishops" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for Bishops">prefect of the Sacred Consistorial Congregation</a>. There was, however, a Cardinal-Secretary to run these bodies on a day-to-day basis.
</p><p>Due to the enduring <a href="/wiki/Roman_Question" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Question">Roman Question</a>, after the announcement of his election to the papacy by the Cardinal Protodeacon, Benedict XV, following in the footsteps of his two most recent predecessors, did not appear on the balcony of St. Peter's basilica overlooking St. Peter's Square to grant the <i><a href="/wiki/Urbi_et_orbi" class="mw-redirect" title="Urbi et orbi">urbi et orbi</a></i> blessing. Benedict XV was crowned in the Sistine Chapel on 6 September 1914, and, also as a form of protest due to the Roman Question, there was no ceremony for the formal possession of the <a href="/wiki/Archbasilica_of_Saint_John_Lateran" title="Archbasilica of Saint John Lateran">Archbasilica of Saint John Lateran</a>.
</p><p>In the 1914 conclave, the cardinals were divided into two factions: the "reactionaries" and the "conservatives". The decade-long campaign waged against Modernism cast a cloud over the conclave, and also ensured on the part of the deceased pontiff that there were fewer moderate and progressive cardinals in the Sacred College. However, the cardinals feared that one of the sides in World War I might potentially influence the conclave to elect a pope that would prove amenable to their side and their positions. While the cardinals expressed relief in the abolition of the veto in 1904, there was still a palpable fear that there would be subtle attempts to exert control over the cardinals, hence, deep suspicion in many cardinals of their European colleagues. During the conclave, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Serafini" title="Domenico Serafini">Domenico Serafini</a> and his faction had enough votes to ensure that Della Chiesa was not elected, particularly since the eighth ballot had Della Chiesa with a majority, though below the two-thirds needed for election.<sup id="cite_ref-SV_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SV-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While Serafini was considered "<i><a href="/wiki/Papabile" title="Papabile">papabile</a></i>" due to his position at the Holy Office, and his alignment with the policies of Pius X, other cardinals such as <a href="/wiki/Andrea_Carlo_Ferrari" title="Andrea Carlo Ferrari">Andrea Carlo Ferrari</a> and <a href="/wiki/D%C3%A9sir%C3%A9-Joseph_Mercier" title="Désiré-Joseph Mercier">Désiré-Joseph Mercier</a> believed that the new pope needed to focus not on Modernism and doctrinal debates, but rather, a softer focus after the somewhat harsh reign of Pius X. To that end, those cardinals turned towards electing the liberal <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Pisa" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Pisa">Archbishop of Pisa</a> <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Maffi" title="Pietro Maffi">Pietro Maffi</a>. Della Chiesa, on the other hand, was wedged between Maffi and Serafini as representing the best and worst of both factions, hence his contention in the balloting. While Della Chiesa seemed to secure support from some conservative cardinals, Maffi lagged considerably and hence Serafini was positioned as Della Chiesa's main rival. Since Maffi had lagged in the balloting, his supporters decided to counter Serafini and threw their support for Della Chiesa, securing his election by only one vote in what was a protracted conclave.<sup id="cite_ref-SV_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SV-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-RT_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RT-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Upon his election, Benedict XV was unable to locate a suitable cassock to wear since the three that were available to him did not appropriately fit his frail form. From a diary that recorded the events of the conclave, "the papal habit that was chosen was the small one, but lacking in some part, and being a little long, it was adapted with clothespin pins, and raised and covered with a gold tassel sash".<sup id="cite_ref-RT_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RT-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Peace_efforts">Peace efforts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Peace efforts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg/200px-PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="115" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg/300px-PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg/400px-PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg 2x" data-file-width="915" data-file-height="524" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:PioXIIgernamia1917.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Eugenio_Pacelli" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugenio Pacelli">Eugenio Pacelli</a> at the Imperial Headquarters with the peace proposal of Benedict XV to Emperor <a href="/wiki/Wilhelm_II,_German_Emperor" title="Wilhelm II, German Emperor">Wilhelm II</a></div></div></div>
<p>Benedict XV's pontificate was dominated by <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, which he termed, along with its turbulent aftermath, "the suicide of Europe."<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Benedict's first encyclical extended a heartfelt plea for an end to hostilities. His early call for a general <a href="/wiki/Christmas_truce" title="Christmas truce">Christmas truce</a> in 1914 was ignored, although informal truces were organized. Late in the war, in May–October 1917, the <a href="/wiki/Marian_apparition" title="Marian apparition">apparitions</a> of <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Fatima" class="mw-redirect" title="Our Lady of Fatima">Our Lady of Fatima</a> occurred in <a href="/wiki/Fatima,_Portugal" class="mw-redirect" title="Fatima, Portugal">Fatima, Portugal</a>, apparitions that would be declared "worthy of belief" in 1930 during the papacy of his successor, <a href="/wiki/Pius_XI" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XI">Pius XI</a>.
</p><p>The war and its consequences were Benedict's main focus during the early years of his pontificate. He declared the neutrality of the Holy See and attempted from that perspective to mediate peace in 1916 and 1917. Both sides rejected his initiatives.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg/220px-Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="238" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg/330px-Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg/440px-Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="888" data-file-height="960" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Papa_Benedetto_XV.jpeg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pope Benedict XV during his reign.</div></div></div>
<p>The national antagonisms between the warring parties were accentuated by religious differences before the war, with France, Italy and Belgium being largely Catholic. Vatican relations with Great Britain were good, while neither <a href="/wiki/Prussia" title="Prussia">Prussia</a> nor <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Germany" class="mw-redirect" title="Imperial Germany">Imperial Germany</a> had any official relations with the Vatican.  In Protestant circles of Germany, the notion was popular that the Catholic Pope was neutral on paper only, strongly favoring the allies instead.<sup id="cite_ref-Conrad_Gröber_1937_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conrad_Gröber_1937-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Benedict was said to have prompted <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a> to go to war in order to weaken the German war machine. Also, allegedly, the Papal Nuncio in Paris explained in a meeting of the <i>Institut Catholique</i>, "to fight against France is to fight against God",<sup id="cite_ref-Conrad_Gröber_1937_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conrad_Gröber_1937-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> and the Pope was said to have exclaimed that he was sorry not to be a Frenchman.<sup id="cite_ref-Conrad_Gröber_1937_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conrad_Gröber_1937-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The Belgian Cardinal <a href="/wiki/D%C3%A9sir%C3%A9-Joseph_Mercier" title="Désiré-Joseph Mercier">Désiré-Joseph Mercier</a>, known as a brave patriot during German occupation but also famous for his anti-German propaganda, was said to have been favored by Benedict XV for his enmity to the German cause. After the war, Benedict also allegedly praised the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Versailles" title="Treaty of Versailles">Treaty of Versailles</a>, which humiliated the Germans.<sup id="cite_ref-Conrad_Gröber_1937_29-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Conrad_Gröber_1937-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>These allegations were rejected by the Vatican's <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a> <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Gasparri" title="Pietro Gasparri">Pietro Gasparri</a>, who wrote on 4 March 1916 that the Holy See is completely impartial and does not favor the allied side. This was even more important, so Gasparri noted, after the diplomatic representatives of Germany and Austria-Hungary to the Vatican were expelled from Rome by Italian authorities.<sup id="cite_ref-Gröber_495_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gröber_495-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> However, considering all this, German Protestants rejected any "Papal Peace", calling it insulting. French politician <a href="/wiki/Georges_Clemenceau" title="Georges Clemenceau">Georges Clemenceau</a>, a fierce anti-clerical, claimed to regard the Vatican initiative as anti-French. Benedict made many unsuccessful attempts to negotiate peace, but these pleas for a negotiated peace made him unpopular, even in Catholic countries like Italy, among many supporters of the war who were determined to accept nothing less than total victory.<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard,_136_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard,_136-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 1 August 1917, Benedict issued a seven-point peace plan stating that:
</p>
<ol><li>"the moral force of right… be substituted for the material force of arms,"</li>
<li>there must be "simultaneous and reciprocal diminution of armaments,"</li>
<li>a mechanism for "international arbitration must be established,"</li>
<li>"true liberty and common rights over the sea" should exist,</li>
<li>there should be a "renunciation of war indemnities,"</li>
<li>occupied territories should be evacuated, and</li>
<li>there should be "an examination… of rival claims."</li></ol>
<p>Great Britain reacted favorably though popular opinion was mixed.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> United States <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_United_States" title="President of the United States">President</a> <a href="/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson" title="Woodrow Wilson">Woodrow Wilson</a> rejected the plan. Bulgaria and Austria-Hungary were also favorable, but Germany replied ambiguously.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Benedict also called for outlawing conscription,<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> a call he repeated in 1921.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some of the proposals eventually were included in Woodrow Wilson's <a href="/wiki/Fourteen_Points" title="Fourteen Points">Fourteen Points</a> call for peace in January 1918.<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard,_136_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard,_136-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In Europe, each side saw him as biased in favor of the other and was unwilling to accept the terms he proposed. Still, although unsuccessful, his diplomatic efforts during the war are credited with an increase of papal prestige and served as a model in the 20th century for the peace efforts of <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a> before and during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, the policies of <a href="/wiki/Paul_VI" class="mw-redirect" title="Paul VI">Paul VI</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Vietnam_War" title="Vietnam War">Vietnam War</a>, and the position of <a href="/wiki/John_Paul_II" class="mw-redirect" title="John Paul II">John Paul II</a> before and during the <a href="/wiki/Iraq_War" title="Iraq War">Iraq War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard,_136_31-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard,_136-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In addition to his efforts in the field of international diplomacy Pope Benedict also tried to bring about peace through Christian faith, as he published a special prayer in 1915 to be spoken by Catholics throughout the world.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> There is a statue in Saint Peter's Basilica of the Pontiff absorbed in prayer, kneeling on a tomb which commemorates a fallen soldier of the war, which he described as a "useless massacre".
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Humanitarian_efforts">Humanitarian efforts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Humanitarian efforts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:232px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pio1917.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Pio1917.jpg/230px-Pio1917.jpg" decoding="async" width="230" height="184" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Pio1917.jpg/345px-Pio1917.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Pio1917.jpg/460px-Pio1917.jpg 2x" data-file-width="902" data-file-height="721" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pio1917.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Nuncio <a href="/wiki/Eugenio_Pacelli" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugenio Pacelli">Eugenio Pacelli</a> delivers packages from Benedict XV to Italian POWs in 1917</div></div></div><p> Almost from the beginning of the war, November 1914, Benedict negotiated with the warring parties about an exchange of wounded and other prisoners of war who were unable to continue fighting. Tens of thousands of such prisoners were exchanged through his intervention.<sup id="cite_ref-Gröber_495_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gröber_495-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> On 15 January 1915, he proposed an exchange of civilians from the occupied zones, which resulted in 20,000 persons being sent to unoccupied Southern France in one month.<sup id="cite_ref-Gröber_495_30-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gröber_495-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> In 1916, Benedict managed to hammer out an agreement between both sides by which 29,000 prisoners with lung disease from the gas attacks could be sent into Switzerland.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> In May 1918, he also negotiated an agreement whereby prisoners on both sides with at least 18 months of captivity and four children at home would also be sent to neutral Switzerland.<sup id="cite_ref-Gröber_495_30-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gröber_495-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He succeeded in 1915 in reaching an agreement by which the warring parties promised not to let prisoners of war (POWs) work on Sundays and <a href="/wiki/Religious_holiday" class="mw-redirect" title="Religious holiday">holidays</a>. Several individuals on both sides were spared the death penalty after his intervention. <a href="/wiki/Hostage" title="Hostage">Hostages</a> were exchanged and corpses repatriated.<sup id="cite_ref-Gröber_495_30-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gröber_495-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The Pope founded the <i>Opera dei Prigionieri</i> to assist in distributing information on prisoners. By the end of the war, some 600,000 items of correspondence were processed by the Vatican. Almost a third of it concerned missing persons. Some 40,000 people had asked for help in the repatriation of sick POWs and 50,000 letters were sent from families to their loved ones who were POWs.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Both during and after the war, Benedict was primarily concerned about the fate of the children, on whose behalf he issued an encyclical. In 1916 he appealed to the people and clergy of the United States to help him feed the starving children in German-occupied Belgium. His aid to children was not limited to Belgium but extended to children in Lithuania, Poland, Lebanon, Montenegro, Syria and Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Benedict was particularly appalled at the new military invention of aerial warfare and protested several times against it to no avail.<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard_116_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard_116-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:402px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg/400px-Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg" decoding="async" width="400" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg/600px-Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg/800px-Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3189" data-file-height="1181" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Wilson_to_Benedict_English.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Excerpt from the letter of <a href="/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson" title="Woodrow Wilson">Woodrow Wilson</a> to Benedict XV.<br /> 24 Dec 1918</div></div></div>
<p>In May and June 1915, the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a> waged a <a href="/wiki/Armenian_genocide" title="Armenian genocide">genocide against the Armenian Christian minorities</a> in <a href="/wiki/Anatolia" title="Anatolia">Anatolia</a>. The Vatican attempted to get Germany and Austria-Hungary involved in protesting to its Turkish ally. The Pope himself sent a personal letter to the Sultan, who was also <a href="/wiki/Caliph" class="mw-redirect" title="Caliph">Caliph</a> of Islam. It had no success "as over a million Armenians died, either killed outright by the Turks or from maltreatment or starvation".<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard_116_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard_116-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="After_the_war">After the war</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: After the war">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>At the time, the anti-Vatican resentment, combined with Italian diplomatic moves to isolate the Vatican in light of the unresolved Roman Question,<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> contributed to the exclusion of the Vatican from the <a href="/wiki/Paris_Peace_Conference,_1919" class="mw-redirect" title="Paris Peace Conference, 1919">Paris Peace conference</a> of 1919 (although it was also part of a historical pattern of political and diplomatic marginalization of the papacy after the loss of the Papal States). Despite this, he wrote an <a href="/wiki/Encyclical" title="Encyclical">encyclical</a> pleading for international reconciliation, <i>Pacem, Dei Munus Pulcherrimum.</i><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After the war, Benedict focused the Vatican's activities on overcoming famine and misery in Europe and establishing contacts and relations with the many new states which were created because of the demise of Imperial Russia, Austria-Hungary, and Germany. Large food shipments, and information about and contacts with POWs, were to be the first steps for a better understanding of the papacy in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-Franzen_380_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Franzen_380-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Regarding the <a href="/wiki/Versailles_Peace_Conference" class="mw-redirect" title="Versailles Peace Conference">Versailles Peace Conference</a>, the Vatican believed that the economic conditions imposed on Germany were too harsh and threatened the European economic stability as a whole. Cardinal Gasparri believed that the peace conditions and the humiliation of the Germans would likely result in another war as soon as Germany would be militarily in a position to start one.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> The Vatican also rejected the dissolution of <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>, seeing in this step an inevitable and eventual strengthening of Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The Vatican also had great reservations about the creation of small successor states which, in the view of Gasparri, were not viable economically and therefore condemned to economic misery.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Benedict rejected the <a href="/wiki/League_of_Nations" title="League of Nations">League of Nations</a> as a secular organization that was not built on Christian values.<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard_147_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard_147-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> On the other hand, he also condemned European nationalism that was rampant in the 1920s and asked for "European Unification" in his 1920 encyclical <a href="https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacem_Dei_Munus_Pulcherrimum" class="extiw" title="it:Pacem Dei Munus Pulcherrimum"><i>Pacem Dei Munus Pulcherrimum</i></a>, "peace, a beautiful gift of God".<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard_147_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard_147-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Similarly, Benedict XV extolled the virtues of peace, denouncing the fragility of a peace that is not wholly oriented towards reconciliation. Prophetically, the Pope wrote that "if almost everywhere the war somehow ended and some peace pacts were signed, the germs of ancient grudges still remain. No peace has value if hatred and enmities are not put down together by means of a reconciliation based on mutual charity".<sup id="cite_ref-RT_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RT-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The pope was also disturbed by the communist revolution in Russia. He reacted with horror to the strongly anti-religious policies adopted by <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Lenin" title="Vladimir Lenin">Vladimir Lenin</a>'s government along with the bloodshed and widespread famine which occurred during the subsequent <a href="/wiki/Russian_Civil_War" title="Russian Civil War">Russian Civil War</a>. He undertook the greatest efforts trying to help the victims of the Russian famine with millions in relief.<sup id="cite_ref-Pollard_147_48-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pollard_147-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Following the dissolution of the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a>, concerns were raised in the Vatican about the safety and future of the Catholics in the Holy Land.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Diplomatic_agenda">Diplomatic agenda</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Diplomatic agenda">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:182px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/8/8f/BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg/180px-BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="218" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/8/8f/BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg/270px-BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/8/8f/BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg/360px-BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg 2x" data-file-width="699" data-file-height="846" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BenedictXVferrata1914.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Under Pope Benedict XV Cardinals <a href="/wiki/Domenico_Ferrata" title="Domenico Ferrata">Domenico Ferrata</a> (above) and <a href="/wiki/Rafael_Merry_del_Val" title="Rafael Merry del Val">Rafael Merry del Val</a> in 1914 switched positions in the Vatican</div></div></div>
<p>In the post-war period, Pope Benedict XV was involved in developing the Church administration to deal with the new international system that had emerged. The papacy was faced with the emergence of numerous new states such as Poland, Lithuania, Estonia, <a href="/wiki/Yugoslavia" title="Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Czechoslovakia" title="Czechoslovakia">Czechoslovakia</a>, Finland, and others. Germany, France, Italy, and Austria were impoverished from the war. In addition, the traditional social and cultural European order was threatened by right-wing nationalism and fascism as well as left-wing socialism and communism, all of which potentially threatened the existence and freedom of the Church. To deal with these and related issues, Benedict engaged in what he knew best, a large scale diplomatic offensive to secure the rights of the faithful in all countries.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Italy">Italy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Italy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Leo_XIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Leo XIII">Leo XIII</a> already had agreed to the participation of Catholics in local but not national politics. Relations with Italy improved as well under Benedict XV, who de facto reversed the stiff anti-Italian policy of his predecessors by allowing Catholics to participate in national elections as well. This led to a surgence of the <a href="/wiki/Italian_People%27s_Party_(1919)" title="Italian People&#39;s Party (1919)">Partito Popolare Italiano</a> under <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Sturzo" title="Luigi Sturzo">Luigi Sturzo</a>. Anti-Catholic politicians were gradually replaced by persons who were neutral or even sympathetic to the Catholic Church. The King of Italy himself gave signals of his desire for better relations, when, for example, he sent personal condolences to the Pontiff on the death of his brother.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> The working conditions for Vatican staff greatly improved and feelers were extended on both sides to solve the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Question" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Question">Roman Question</a>. Benedict XV strongly supported a solution and seemed to have had a fairly pragmatic view of the political and social situation in Italy at this time.
</p><p>Benedict XV, along with most traditional Catholics of his time, opposed voting rights for women in principle, on the grounds that it would take them out of their "natural sphere".<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> However, he was pragmatic and recognized female suffrage could be a "social necessity in some countries...in order to counter the generally subversive views of the socialists with the supposedly conservative votes of women", believing that women would help support traditional Catholic positions if granted suffrage.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="France">France</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: France">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:192px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg/190px-Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg" decoding="async" width="190" height="251" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg/285px-Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg/380px-Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg 2x" data-file-width="802" data-file-height="1060" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Scherrer_jeanne_enters_orlean.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Joan of Arc enters Orléans</i> (painting by J.J. Sherer, 1887). Joan was canonized by Pope Benedict XV in 1920.</div></div></div>
<p>Benedict XV also attempted to improve relations with the anti-clerical Republican government of France, and canonized the French national heroine <a href="/wiki/Joan_of_Arc" title="Joan of Arc">Saint Joan of Arc</a>. In the mission territories of the <a href="/wiki/Third_World" title="Third World">Third World</a>, he emphasized the necessity of training native priests to quickly replace the European missionaries, and founded the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Oriental_Institute" title="Pontifical Oriental Institute">Pontifical Oriental Institute</a> and the Coptic College in the Vatican. Pius XI would entrust the "Orientale" to the <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a> and make it a part of the Jesuit's <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_Consortium" title="Gregorian Consortium">Gregorian Consortium</a> in Rome (along with the <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregorian University">Gregorian University</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Biblicum" class="mw-redirect" title="Biblicum">Biblicum</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> In 1921, France re-established diplomatic relations with the Vatican.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Soviet_Union">Soviet Union</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Soviet Union">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93Soviet_Union_relations" title="Holy See–Soviet Union relations">Holy See–Soviet Union relations</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:182px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pietro_Gasparri.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Pietro_Gasparri.jpg/180px-Pietro_Gasparri.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="251" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Pietro_Gasparri.jpg/270px-Pietro_Gasparri.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Pietro_Gasparri.jpg/360px-Pietro_Gasparri.jpg 2x" data-file-width="685" data-file-height="955" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pietro_Gasparri.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a> <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Gasparri" title="Pietro Gasparri">Pietro Gasparri</a></div></div></div>
<p>The end of the war caused the revolutionary development, which Benedict XV had foreseen in his first encyclical. With the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Revolution_(1917)" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian Revolution (1917)">Russian Revolution</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Vatican</a> was faced with a new, so far unknown, situation.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Lithuania_and_Estonia">Lithuania and Estonia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Lithuania and Estonia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:147px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rampolla_1913.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/d8/Rampolla_1913.jpg/145px-Rampolla_1913.jpg" decoding="async" width="145" height="201" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/d8/Rampolla_1913.jpg/218px-Rampolla_1913.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/d8/Rampolla_1913.jpg/290px-Rampolla_1913.jpg 2x" data-file-width="748" data-file-height="1036" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rampolla_1913.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>His friend Cardinal Rampolla at the age of 70, shortly before his death</div></div></div>
<p>The relations with Russia changed drastically for a second reason. The <a href="/wiki/Baltic_states" title="Baltic states">Baltic states</a> and Poland gained their independence from Russia after <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, thus enabling a relatively free Church life in those former Russia-controlled countries. Estonia was the first country to look for <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Vatican</a> ties. On 11 April 1919, <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a> Pietro Gasparri informed the Estonian authorities that the Vatican would agree to have diplomatic relations. A <a href="/wiki/Concordat" title="Concordat">concordat</a> was agreed upon in principle a year later in June 1920. It was signed on 30 May 1922. It guaranteed freedom for the Catholic Church, established archdioceses, liberated clergy from military service, allowed the creation of seminaries and Catholic schools, and enshrined church property rights and immunity. The Archbishop swore alliance to Estonia.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Relations with Catholic Lithuania were slightly more complicated because of the Polish <a href="/wiki/Military_occupation" title="Military occupation">occupation</a> of <a href="/wiki/Vilnius" title="Vilnius">Vilnius</a>, a city and archiepiscopal seat, which Lithuania claimed as its own. Polish forces had occupied Vilnius and committed acts of <a href="/wiki/Police_brutality" title="Police brutality">brutality</a> in its Catholic <a href="/wiki/Seminary" title="Seminary">seminary</a> there.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> This generated several protests by Lithuania to the Holy See.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Relations with the Holy See were defined during the pontificate of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> (1922–1939).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Poland">Poland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Poland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Before all other heads of state, Pope Benedict XV in October 1918 congratulated the Polish people on their independence.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidlin_III,_306_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidlin_III,_306-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> In a public letter to Archbishop Kakowski of <a href="/wiki/Warsaw" title="Warsaw">Warsaw</a>, he remembered their loyalty and the many efforts of the Holy See to assist them. He expressed his hopes that Poland would again take its place in the family of nations and continue its history as an educated Christian nation.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidlin_III,_306_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidlin_III,_306-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> In March 1919, he nominated 10 new bishops and, soon after, <a href="/wiki/Achille_Ratti" class="mw-redirect" title="Achille Ratti">Achille Ratti</a>, who was already in Warsaw as his representative, as papal nuncio.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidlin_III,_306_56-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidlin_III,_306-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> He repeatedly cautioned Polish authorities against persecuting Lithuanian and <a href="/wiki/Ruthenian_Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Ruthenian Greek Catholic Church">Ruthenian</a> clergy.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidlin_III,_307_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidlin_III,_307-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> During the Bolshevik advance against Warsaw, he asked for worldwide public prayers for Poland. <a href="/wiki/Nuncio" title="Nuncio">Nuncio</a> Ratti was the only foreign diplomat to stay in the Polish capital. On 11 June 1921, he wrote to the Polish episcopate, warning against political misuses of spiritual power, urging again for peaceful coexistence with neighbouring peoples, stating that "love of country has its limits in justice and obligations".<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> He sent nuncio Ratti to Silesia to act against potential political agitations of the Catholic clergy.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidlin_III,_307_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidlin_III,_307-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ratti, a scholar, intended to work for Poland and build bridges to the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>, hoping even to shed his blood for Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-Stehle_25_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stehle_25-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Pope Benedict XV needed him as a diplomat and not as a <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">martyr</a> and forbade any trip into the <a href="/wiki/USSR" class="mw-redirect" title="USSR">USSR</a> even though he was the official papal delegate to Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-Stehle_25_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stehle_25-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> However, he continued his contacts with Russia. This did not generate much sympathy for him within Poland at the time. He was asked to go. While he tried honestly to show himself as a friend of Poland, Warsaw forced his departure after his neutrality in <a href="/wiki/Silesia" title="Silesia">Silesian</a> voting was questioned<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> by Germans and Poles. Nationalistic Germans objected to a Polish nuncio supervising elections, and Poles were upset because he curtailed agitating clergy.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidlin_IV,_15_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidlin_IV,_15-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> On 20 November, when German Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Adolf_Bertram" title="Adolf Bertram">Adolf Bertram</a> announced a papal ban on all political activities of clergymen, calls for Ratti's expulsion climaxed in Warsaw.<sup id="cite_ref-Schmidlin_IV,_15_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Schmidlin_IV,_15-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Two years later, Achille Ratti became <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a>, shaping Vatican policies towards Poland with Pietro Gasparri and <a href="/wiki/Eugenio_Pacelli" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugenio Pacelli">Eugenio Pacelli</a> for the following 36 years (1922–1958).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Israel">Israel</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Israel">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As part of the preliminary diplomatic negotiations leading to the <a href="/wiki/Balfour_Declaration" title="Balfour Declaration">Balfour Declaration</a>, Pope Benedict gave his support to a Jewish homeland in Palestine to Zionist diplomat <a href="/wiki/Nahum_Sokolow" title="Nahum Sokolow">Nahum Sokolow</a> on 4 May 1917, describing the return of the Jews to Palestine as "providential; God has willed it."<sup id="cite_ref-KramerInternationalLaw_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KramerInternationalLaw-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="United_States">United States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: United States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg/220px-Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="317" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg/330px-Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg/440px-Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2046" data-file-height="2952" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedict_xv_pontifex.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Portrait of Benedict XV by <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Fabr%C3%A9s" title="Antonio Fabrés">Antonio Fabrés</a> (1916, <a href="/wiki/Museo_del_Prado" title="Museo del Prado">Museo del Prado</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>Cardinal <a href="/wiki/James_Gibbons" title="James Gibbons">James Gibbons</a> helped secure a <a href="/wiki/List_of_meetings_between_the_pope_and_the_president_of_the_United_States" title="List of meetings between the pope and the president of the United States">meeting between the pope and President Woodrow Wilson</a> which took place on 4 January 1919. The cardinal had sent a letter to the President imploring him to visit the pope after learning that Wilson was to go to Europe. Not long after, Wilson confirmed the visit and went to meet the pope accompanied by the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_North_American_College" title="Pontifical North American College">Pontifical North American College</a> rector Charles O'Hearn. Benedict XV took Wilson by the hand and led him into the study for their meeting, with the pope later presenting Wilson with a gift: a <a href="/wiki/Mosaic" title="Mosaic">mosaic</a> of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Saint Peter</a>. The interpreter had to be present for the meeting, since the pope was speaking in French, and Wilson only spoke English. The presidential party was presented to the pope, and after presenting his personal physician Admiral <a href="/wiki/Cary_T._Grayson" title="Cary T. Grayson">Grayson</a> (telling the pope that he "is the man who keeps me well"), the pope said: "Apparently, he has done a splendid job", before offering words to Grayson. The pope blessed the entourage, despite Wilson's slight confusion, after the pope assured Wilson his blessing did not discriminate those of other faiths, since Wilson was a <a href="/wiki/Presbyterian" class="mw-redirect" title="Presbyterian">Presbyterian</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mag_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mag-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, friction remained between the two during their private audience. Benedict XV wanted to be involved in the discussions at Versailles, but Wilson disagreed with this.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ireland">Ireland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Ireland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Easter_Rising" title="Easter Rising">Easter Rising</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Irish_Treaty" title="Anglo-Irish Treaty">Anglo-Irish Treaty</a></div> 
<p>Following the declaration of the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Republic" title="Irish Republic">Irish Republic</a> in 1919, Benedict XV was visited by <a href="/wiki/Se%C3%A1n_T._O%27Kelly" title="Seán T. O&#39;Kelly">Seán T. O'Kelly</a> in May 1920, who explained Irish politics and presented a memorandum, hoping for support from the "Sovereign Pontiff".<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In 1921, <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Irish_Treaty" title="Anglo-Irish Treaty">a peace agreement</a> was formalized between the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British Empire</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Republic" title="Irish Republic">Irish Republic</a> that saw to the end of the <a href="/wiki/Irish_War_of_Independence" title="Irish War of Independence">Irish War of Independence</a>. Negotiations were conducted in <a href="/wiki/London" title="London">London</a>, however, there was a rise in tension during the negotiations over separate telegrams sent to Benedict XV by both <a href="/wiki/George_V" title="George V">King George V</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C3%89amon_de_Valera" title="Éamon de Valera">Éamon de Valera</a>. The telegram sent by de Valera took issue with the language that George V used in his message to the pope and responded to simply clarify the nature between both parties and the reasons that there had been tension between the two.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> In his letter to George V, the Pope praised the ongoing efforts for peace, saying that "We rejoice at the resumption of the Anglo-Irish negotiations" and that the negotiations would "end the age-long dissention" between both sides.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Benedict XV further declared himself "overjoyed with the agreement happily reached in regard to Ireland".<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A source of contention, despite firm denials by the Holy See in 1933, is the allegation that Benedict XV, in a private interview with <a href="/wiki/Papal_count" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal count">Count</a> <a href="/wiki/George_Noble_Plunkett" title="George Noble Plunkett">George Noble Plunkett</a> in mid-April 1916, imparted an <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_blessing" title="Apostolic blessing">Apostolic Blessing</a> to the Irish Republic just two weeks before the <a href="/wiki/Easter_Rising" title="Easter Rising">Easter Rising</a>. The fact that the Pope is purported to have done so was widely accepted by <a href="/wiki/Irish_republicanism" title="Irish republicanism">Republicans</a>, though categorically denied later by <i><a href="/wiki/L%27Osservatore_Romano" title="L&#39;Osservatore Romano">L'Osservatore Romano</a></i> in 1933. Additionally, it was alleged that Plunkett pledged the Irish Republic to fidelity to the Holy See, with the Pope formally imparting his blessing on the endeavors of the Irish Republic, prompting the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Armagh" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Armagh">Archbishop of Armagh</a> <a href="/wiki/Michael_Logue" title="Michael Logue">Michael Logue</a> to send a telegram to the Pope on 30 April 1916 asking for a clarification as to what occurred during the meeting. When <i>L'Osservatore Romano</i> refuted these claims in 1933, the paper said that "the news was entirely unfounded" and that the actions of Benedict XV were "in open contradiction to the well-known gentleness of the late Pontiff and to his most lively desire for peace and the prevention of any further effusion of blood". Plunkett himself affirmed the truth of the allegations, saying "The fact is that the that the Papal Blessing given the men of 1916 by Pope Benedict has only one witness remaining – myself. Those who profess to refute me have not evidence to support them".<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_affairs">Church affairs</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Church affairs">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Theology">Theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XV&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
